<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which domain-level function is associated with the SNT and TXP motifs in this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> They primarily mediate sodium coupling rather than recognition of the transported substrate.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> They primarily promote assembly of the transporter into a dimer.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> They are essential for recognition of the transported substrate.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> They form an ATP-hydrolysis site that powers transport.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence shows characteristics of a Major Facilitator Superfamily (MFS) transporter, specifically from the sugar porter (SP) family, based on its topology with ~12 transmembrane helices and the presence of known family motifs.<br><br>The SNT (Ser-Asn-Thr) and TXP (Thr-X-Pro) motifs are signature motifs of the sugar porter family within the MFS. These motifs are located within the sugar-binding pocket of the transporter:<br><br>- The **TXP motif** is found in the N-terminal 6-TM bundle, typically in transmembrane helix 2.<br>- The **SNT motif** is found in the C-terminal 6-TM bundle, typically in transmembrane helix 8.<br><br>Both motifs line the central substrate-binding cavity and are critical for hydrogen bonding interactions with the transported sugar substrate (e.g., glucose). Mutagenesis studies have shown that altering residues in these motifs significantly impairs substrate recognition and binding.<br><br>Evaluating the other options:<br>- **A** is incorrect because MFS transporters (unlike SLC5 Na⁺-dependent cotransporters) do not use sodium coupling.<br>- **B** is incorrect because these motifs are not involved in oligomerization; they are in the transmembrane core lining the substrate pathway.<br>- **D** is incorrect because MFS transporters are facilitators or secondary active transporters, not ATP-driven pumps (that would be ABC transporters).<br><br>Therefore, the SNT and TXP motifs are essential for recognition of the transported substrate.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Check the SNT and TXP motif candidates against the provided sequence and consider their context. Which domain-level role is associated with these motifs in this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> They primarily mediate sodium coupling rather than recognition of the transported substrate.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> They primarily promote assembly of the transporter into a dimer.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> They are essential for recognition of the transported substrate.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> They form an ATP-hydrolysis site that powers transport.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_molecular_function_0028:1<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence shown is characteristic of an SLC26 family transporter (likely prestin/SLC26A5 based on its length and transmembrane topology). SLC26 transporters are anion exchangers that possess a characteristic transmembrane domain with specific motifs.<br><br>Looking at the sequence for SNT and TXP motif candidates:<br>- The &quot;TAP&quot; region in &quot;GTAPNLILLGQLK&quot; corresponds to the TXP-type motif region (Thr-Ala-Pro)<br>- The &quot;NVT&quot; in &quot;PANTHAANVTALPP&quot; region relates to the SNT-type motif<br><br>In SLC26 family transporters, the SNT (Ser-Asn-Thr) and TXP (Thr-X-Pro) motifs are located within the transmembrane domain and together form the anion-binding pocket. These motifs are critical structural elements that directly coordinate the transported anion substrate. Mutagenesis studies on SLC26 proteins (including prestin, pendrin, and SLC26A9) have demonstrated that mutations in these motifs abolish anion binding without necessarily eliminating the transport mechanism itself.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- A: Sodium coupling is not the primary role; these are anion transporters, and the motifs are specifically for substrate (anion) recognition.<br>- B: Dimer assembly is mediated by the STAS domain, not the SNT/TXP motifs.<br>- C: This is correct — these motifs form the substrate (anion) recognition site.<br>- D: SLC26 proteins are not ATPases; they use secondary active transport or direct exchange mechanisms, so there is no ATP-hydrolysis site.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
